## 397 — Camera-coherent wall cutaway for the complete Studio

Previous shader preparation fix was progress. Implemented smooth foreground-wall fading in the common3D panel. Camera/focus signed distances to calibrated wall planes drive a continuous fade (including diagonal walls), so adjacent cells on the same plane agree instead of switching tile by tile. Exponential temporal easing avoids an abrupt orbit transition. Walls retain3% opacity at full fade; this is view transparency, not geometry removal or a modeled section cut. Added Walls:auto/solid control; showing roofs restores full walls for exterior inspection.

Mapped opening frames/glass, wall finish strips and mounted fixtures/art/curtains to their authoritative host wall so they fade together. Geometry, cast shadows, physics and saved scene data remain unchanged. Faded foreground meshes are excluded from direct ray selection so objects behind can be selected; solid mode restores wall picking. Room radiance capture temporarily restores authored opacity and depth writes, then restores camera-view opacity, keeping full architecture in diffuse/reflection capture. Roof cutaway behavior retained.

## 410 — Bounded second diffuse bounce with coherent capture state

Previous turn changed the Studio media wall and verified an actual passage. This turn improves the shared indirect-light pipeline rather than changing more isolated props. Original source textures and all geometry remain unchanged. Room capture now performs two complete generations: pass1 captures direct-lit surface radiance; pass2 captures direct plus pass1 diffuse irradiance. Each pass2 face uses the entire completed pass1 field, never partially updated rooms. Existing local PMREM reflections are generated only from final-pass radiance; diffuse is still supplied once through SH, not duplicated through IBL.

Added probe-field-state.js to temporarily install capture uniforms and restore every published vector/count synchronously before yielding to gameplay, including on exceptions. Existing generation cancellation and final publication stay in place. First-pass intermediate fields therefore cannot flash into the normal view. No arbitrary exposure increase or additive old-frame accumulation. This remains a bounded approximate two-bounce study, with room masks and inverse-distance interpolation; no within-room visibility correction, parallax correction or UE5/Lumen parity is claimed.

Real browser retained old loaded module for night-before.png, then reloaded and repeated Frame room/Moonlight for night-after.png. Both use scene revision86,1280x720,default room framing,lamps on,roof cutaway,automatic walls. Comparison shows a modest warmer fill on wall/bathroom surfaces, not a transformative fidelity improvement. Night-off.png shows warm contributions disappear after recapture. Daylight also rebuilt successfully. Actual capture duration single574ms versus two1131ms; lamps-off947ms and daylight1302ms. These are total asynchronous update durations, not one-frame stalls. Rendering sample overlaps the daylight capture:120samples CPU p50 8.3ms,p95 9.9ms; frame cadence p50 8.3ms,p95 17ms;2148draw calls,506256triangles. GPU duration and memory bytes remain unmeasured. No browser warning/error captured.

## 418 — Window-visible sky transport and a coordinated domestic palette

Once room transport is active, indoor fragments subtract the hemisphere fallback before adding captured irradiance. Outdoors and disabled-study comparisons retain the fallback. ## 422 — Soft upholstery, contact poses, and curved-receiver shadow failure

User requested soft fabric sofas/beds and physically plausible sitting/lying. Added reusable real-time critically damped foam response, bounded top depression/peripheral bulge, immutable source geometry restoration, and dynamic batching exclusion. Declared soft support/cover/overlay metadata in recipe output and retained source cloth textures/fibre material. Replaced bed rigid duvet/throw boxes with folded, draped textile surfaces. Bed covers and the sofa throw follow the nearest support's actual triangle displacement; cached binding avoids repeated heavy setup. Approximate undeformed hulls still provide conservative movement collision. This is visual foam/contact animation, NOT cloth dynamics, tissue/pressure physics, or solved whole-body contact.

Rest/sleep use existing reachable game facilities/timed actions. Rest metadata binds the same scene object; source save unchanged. Added procedural imported-skeleton posing, body anchoring, knee/ankle and hand constraints. Sleeping follows the bed axis; sitting uses the approach-side bed edge or sofa front lip. Uses real seconds despite accelerated survival time. Stopping test walk resets geometry; walking cancels rest and lets compression recover. Source directional mesh transforms remain authoritative.

Browser failures drove corrections: bed-centre sitting put shins into the mattress; deep-chaise centre sitting did the same. Moved sitting anchors to edges. Mirrored skeleton world quaternion aiming was wrong; moved aiming into parent-local coordinates. Torso initially faced opposite knees; corrected source heading and checked actual skin. Hands initially penetrated the cushion; added forearm targets. Preserved screenshots. Dense throw vs sparse cushion led to triangle binding rather than assuming identical analytic displacement means identical surfaces. Render vertices were split, so naive normal recomputation produced flat facets; normal smoothing now groups coincident source vertices. Remaining severe mottling was NOT solved by those geometry changes: actual day/night browser comparison isolated planar receiver-gradient shadow extrapolation on curved cloth. Curved soft materials retain 24-tap filtering without that extrapolation. Final sofa screenshot visibly eliminates mottled triangles. No light intensity increase was used to hide them.

Actual UI verification: Workshop → 3D → temporary visitor; walk workstation to sofa, normal Inventory → Rest, choose 1x for observation, orbit/zoom; final sofa-contact-final.png. Movement cancels rest; sofa-released.png records return. Reopen preview and walk to bed; Sleep then cancellation and Rest via normal facilities; bed-sleep-final.png and bed-rest-final.png. Bedroom remains very dark at the inspected night angle; this is NOT full bedroom lighting or human-contact visual acceptance. Minor draped-edge penetration remains in geometric diagnostic (6 sampled vertices above z .44 had up to ~8 mm overlap at the draped edge); do not call the layered cloth a general collision solution. Sleeping has only a procedural straight pose; no blanket covering/pulling, distributed shoulder/hip pressure, weight-dependent foam, or fully solved entry/exit animation. These remain needed for the user's realistic-touch quality target.

Canonical volume and asset rebuild verification passed. Full regression suite 893 tests passed including spring recovery, bounded compression, object isolation/reset, bed/chaise anchor rules, mirrored leg aim, dense-overlay binding, normal continuity, and curved shadow shader specialization. Updated authoring field guide with reusable lessons. Browser preview opening initially timed out during expensive binding; inspected the same page and confirmed it had opened without duplicate action; cached coefficients/vertex bindings and final opening completed normally. Persistent injured resident remained paused; only temporary visitors advanced. No three-day residence rerun, no deployment, no overall goal-complete claim.

Subsequent browser logs established the actual cause: `FRAGMENT shader texture image units count exceeds MAX_TEXTURE_IMAGE_UNITS(16)` for Standard and Physical materials. Changing light type alone did not fix it. Preserved failed-sampler-limit.png. Added shared fixtureShadowBudget: reserve3 surface maps+1 reflection+1 sun+1 television+1 container shadow before allocating repeated fixture samples;16-unit browser now permits9 fixture shadow samples instead of10. Does not delete material maps, turn off major light sources, remove food families or weaken required visual scope. Stable interactive-light allocation remains useful independently. Added regression covering16-unit capacity and insufficient hardware rejection. Actual browser retest required below.

The first budget correction still failed actual rendering. Source inspection of Three's `lights_physical_pars_fragment.glsl.js` found the omitted `uniform sampler2D dfgLUT`, shared by Standard/Physical lighting. Added that reserved sampler, reducing the16-unit fixture budget to8. Kept a shader error diagnostic logging actual program errors, enabled defines and sampler declarations for future resource failures. Earlier9-sample calculation is superseded, not accepted evidence.

The8-shadow budget could not support this room's9 distinct fixtures and correctly rejected startup. Rather than remove a lamp or drop texture detail, added lossless opaque RGB+roughness-alpha packing (`packed-surface-maps.js`). Preserves every source RGB byte and each roughness green-channel byte; alpha remains linear in sRGB sampling. Restores material opacity in the shader, so wall cutaway remains effective. Transparent source pixels are not repacked. Clone sources are detached to avoid mutating textures used by other materials. Room-bounce compilation now chains preexisting material hooks instead of overwriting the packing hook. Two surface samplers + reflection + DFG LUT + sun/TV/container reserve7, allowing all9 fixtures on16-unit hardware. Actual browser now renders walls, opaque fridge and furniture correctly with illuminated contents. Source-map packing is a reusable pipeline improvement, not a lower-fidelity material substitute.

Also found 3D startup rejection fell through into unpaused2D gameplay; it advanced the saved resident from late Day12 to Day13 during diagnosis. Preserved that actual state (no time reset). Fixed startup catch to keep simulation paused and reopen the menu. This interval is NOT counted as successful three-day residence.

Added regression for removal/reinsertion stability.

## 497 — Coalesce room rendering around the live simulation frame

TV decoded-frame callbacks, cutaway fade RAFs and the resident simulation could each submit a whole-room render in the same display interval. Added reusable render-demand scheduler: a running simulation owns synchronous painting; independent requests coalesce into one RAF when paused. Simulation flush cancels any pending independent frame, preserving CPU timing measurements. Fade animation requests another frame only through this scheduler. Disposal cancels pending work. Explicit loop ownership handles window blur even when document.activeElement remains the canvas. Live diagnostics report scheduler counter deltas over the actual sample, not panel lifetime totals.

Full1018 tests passed (/tmp/tian-497-full-tests.txt); after loop-ownership boundary fix, four affected tests passed, including window blur with active canvas, focus resume, paused-request coalescing and disposal. Runtime reload succeeded; real movement changed x7998.700,y11404.478 to7998.342,11404.271. Live TV + simulation sample underway. No geometry/material rebuild or quality claim: this targets redundant rendering, not the outstanding air/material fidelity gap.

## 501 — Video-driven first diffuse bounce

Previous turn500 changed authoritative camera controls and produced actual browser evidence: progress. This turn investigated the user's missing reflected television illumination. Television spotlights/screen were explicitly excluded from static room capture; therefore decoded video affected direct illumination only. Added reusable deterministic geometric transfer for four screen patches: stratified cone rays find opaque surfaces; normal, source falloff, diffuse base albedo and source/surface/probe visibility generate per-patch SH transfer at existing room probes. Decoded quadrant linear RGB multiplies the cached transfer, using the same receiver depth visibility and room masks. TV field is separately disabled during static captures so it cannot be baked in or counted twice. Off/black yields zero; stale computations cancel after rebuild/lighting changes/stop/close. This is one diffuse bounce, not full dynamic GI; base colour approximates texture-average albedo and glass/specular/subsequent bounces are not modelled.

Actual browser first pass found 256 surface hits and 1507 visible surface-to-probe paths but blocked for533.4ms. Reworked preparation into four-ray slices with cancellation. Repeated real FRANCE24 playback in night/lamps-off preview: total717.3ms wall time,517.2ms cumulative CPU,maximum slice10.7ms. Those figures describe this cache preparation only, not frame-time acceptance. Subsequent frames reuse transfer. Full1026 tests passed (/tmp/tian501-final-tests.txt), including albedo/linearity/black/occlusion and incremental equivalence/cancellation. Browser has no current errors. Evidence501/live-transfer.json, tv-on-reflected.png, tv-direct-only.png and tv-off.png record actual programme and controls; live programme frames differ, so on/direct-only images are not a controlled quantitative radiometric comparison. Off.json confirmszero emission. Screenshots show light gone after switch-off; overall visual target remains unmet.

Another actual UX failure: long changing video diagnostic JSON shifted the checkbox during a pointer click. Keyboard toggle verified the comparison; fixed output to a stable six-line scroll region and syntax checked. A shell edit initially used a root-relative path from app cwd and failed; corrected fromroot, no source mutation claimed from failed command. Runtime-only changes need no asset regeneration. ## 505b — Indirect-only ambient occlusion; lighting diagnosis

User asked for AO, less even indoor hemisphere fill and specular environment response, with
fixed-camera day/night before/after. Inspection found all three already present (above), so
the batch corrected and measured them instead of duplicating them.

Change: contact-shading.js now defaults to `indirect` mode. GTAO renders before the scene pass;
every opaque Standard/Lambert/Phong volume/character/fridge-content material samples the
denoised AO buffer (texelFetch at gl_FragCoord) after `aomap_fragment` and multiplies ONLY
indirect diffuse (probe bounce or outdoor hemisphere) and environment specular via Three's
computeSpecularOcclusion. Direct sun/lamp/TV light keeps its own shadow maps instead of being
darkened again by a whole-image blend. Transparent/faded surfaces are skipped; the AO uniform
is enabled only during the main pass, so radiance captures never see it. `legacy` reproduces
the pre-505 blend exactly for comparisons; `off` is the old toggle. Budget reserves one
screen-occlusion sampler (8/16 used with shared atlas). Diagnostic window.pz.volumeView exposes
camera yaw/pitch/radius, contact modes and the GTAO pass; it does not touch scene or save.

Raw AO buffer at old settings (radius.35 thickness.15) was almost uniformly white
(ao-buffer-weak.jpg). Reviewed settings radius.5, thickness1, scale2, distanceExponent2
give legible contact at sofa/cabinet bases, wall-floor joints and sink recess (ao-buffer-tuned).

Fixed-camera comparisons (1024×768 CSS, DPR2, resident paused Day18 13:04; one game minute
elapsed during a reload restart, recorded honestly): day kitchen legacy→indirect mean luma
77.76→76.98; day wide 82.97→82.37; night (Moonlight study) wide 46.6→45.16. Diff images:
green=darker at TV base, sofa, wall bases, toilet, cabinet plinths; red=brighter on faded
cut-away walls/fridge which the old blend wrongly darkened from objects behind them. Effect is
correct but SUBTLE: AO alone does not change the overall flat impression.

Reflections off/on at night (night-kitchen-reflections-*.jpg): sink and hob go black without
them, wood/stone barely change. Specular response exists; glossy-material roughness, not a
missing environment, limits it.

Root cause found for the even look: light inventory at night = hemisphere .45 (outdoor only
once bounce ready), moon .8, five wall sconces intensity14.8 with ~87° half-angle cones and
6m range at 1.63m, four bathroom/kitchen fixtures at9, two lamp pairs at6/2. Near-
hemispherical sconce cones evenly wash walls and floor; there are no distinct lamp pools.
Next correction is a fixture-light recipe batch (narrower/up-down wash, shorter range,
practical-lamp pools, lamp-off comparison), through canonical asset recipes, not a global
dimming filter.

Cost: alternating 6 rounds×15 synchronous renders (gl.finish, 2048×1536 buffer, pane possibly
backgrounded): indirect p50 25.3/p90 47ms, legacy 24.2/42, AO off 14.2/25.1. No regression vs
legacy; AO itself costs ~10ms here and remains part of the open performance gap.
Full suite 1036/1036 passed (one new AO test). No asset rebuild, save edit or deployment.

## 505e — Dark daylight interior: diagnosis and interior eye adaptation

Symptom: lamps off at Day18 13:05, interior floor/sofa rendered pure black (0,0,0) while the
lawn was (94,92,43). Measured the published probe field (new read-only bounce.diagnostics()):
daylight-only SH band-0 luminance 0.006–0.049 per probe (log-average key 0.0156) versus
0.06–0.43 with lamps on (key 0.123). Controlled captures ruled out suspected blockers: window
glass at realistic transmission (opacity .3→.1) changed the field ≈+10%; making the four
curtain panels translucent changed nothing; bypassing depth visibility and turning AO off
changed nothing. The room is 66 m² lit by four small north/west windows, no direct sun at
13:00: a 1–3% daylight factor is physically right. The defect is display: one fixed exposure
tuned for lamp-lit rooms renders that daylight interior as black.

Fix: src/interior-exposure.js derives an interior gain from the PUBLISHED probe field
(key = log-average band-0 luminance; gain = clamp((0.12/key)^0.95, 0.6, 10)), eased over
0.9 s. room-bounce-study multiplies outgoing radiance only for fragments inside a captured
room (`tianInside`), so the sunlit exterior keeps base exposure .95 instead of blowing out.
It changes only when indirect lighting is recaptured (lamp switch, 10-minute bucket, edits),
never per frame, so the camera cannot pump it. Inspector toggle "Interior exposure
adaptation" restores fixed exposure for controlled comparisons. No light energy, extra lamps
or ambient fill were added; GI capture is unchanged.

Bug found and fixed during verification: the gain uniform was also active inside probe
captures, so each capture multiplied measured radiance by the current gain and the next
adaptation step fed back on it (probe luminance jumped 0.015→0.3–4.3, gain oscillated
6.9↔0.6; FAILED-feedback-inflated-capture.jpg). Capture now forces gain 1 per cube face;
regression test added. Earlier glass/curtain readings taken during the feedback were
discarded and repeated.

Results (evidence505/daylight, B camera): day lamps off gain 6.5–6.9 — walls, kitchen units,
bed, desk and bathroom readable, sun patch by the piano; floor at the room centre stays dim
(it sees almost no sky), which is physically plausible. Day lamps on gain 0.98 (reviewed look
unchanged). Night lamps off: key 0.004, gain capped at 10, room stays dark with moonlit
patches. Night lamps on gain 1.18 with lamp pools intact. 1040 JS tests pass.

Limits: gain is per-house interior, not per-room local adaptation; a dark corner inside a
bright room is not lifted. Bar/kitchen centre still lacks a ceiling fixture. No browser
performance run after this change (one extra uniform multiply per interior fragment).

## 505h — Kitchen waste bin and rubbish routine

Problem (505g): the resident carried 13 empty tin cans because the home had no bin. Added a
real rubbish routine using imported item types, nothing deleted:

- Models (studio pipeline, recipe + compressed delivery + catalogue sprite + semantics):
  `studio-pedal-bin` (brushed filleted body, plastic base, liner folded over the rim, hinged
  lid that lifts on storage inspection, pedal) and `studio-wheelie-bin` (tapered moulded body,
  ribbed lid on a rear hinge, handle, axle and wheels). Tiles `container=bin`, capacity 10 /
  50, `TianEmptyContainer`; the wheelie bin also `TianOutdoorBin`. Appended at the end of the
  studio catalogue order so existing atlas regions do not move.
- Logic (src/rubbish.js + Simulation.throwAway/tieBinBag): "Throw away" moves a whole carried
  stack of genuine rubbish (Junk / empty-can tags; fill state part of stack identity — a full
  bottle never joins the empties; tools, food and washable dirty bandages are never offered)
  as ONE timed action whose duration is the sum of the transfers it replaces, re-validating
  each item at completion and refusing to overfill. "Tie bin bag" uses a carried empty
  `Base.Garbagebag` or one use from a carried `Base.Garbagebag_box`, moving the bin contents
  into the bag. A tied full bag is rubbish only for an outdoor bin. Reach/bin checks enforced.
- Default builder places the pedal bin at the end of the sink run and the wheelie bin on the
  entrance court, and stocks a box of bin bags in the prep counter (scratch build verified,
  53 objects). Live house: committed through Build commands (rev 1 place, rev 2 swap bin ↔
  plant so the bin faces the entry corridor), applied with Use as my home (stock migrated,
  resident moved to spawn as documented).
- Door names: the nearby-door list said "Door / Door"; src/door-names.js now derives
  "Entrance door" / "Bathroom door" from the rooms either side of the host wall.

Browser verification with the real resident (Day18 13:14→15:01): threw away 13 cans (bin
2.6/10); tie refused with "Carry a bin bag or a box of bin bags" when none carried; fetched
the box from the counter; tied the bag (box 20→19 uses, bin empty, bag holds 13 cans);
carried it out and threw it into the wheelie bin (bag with 13 cans inside the outdoor bin).
1047/1047 JS tests (6 rubbish + door-name tests), asset regenerate/freshness pass.

Incident (kept, not hidden): the figure standing at the entry all session is a zombie. Going
outside, the resident was attacked (Health 100→28, seven scratches). Fled, returned, closed
the "Door" — which was actually the bathroom door (both were named "Door"), so the zombie
followed inside. Killed it bare-handed (no weapon carried; the bat is in the breakfast-bar
storage), disinfected two wounds with the remaining wipes, bandaged six with the carried dirty

This led directly to the door-naming fix. Earlier timed actions were also cancelled by

Open: no corpse handling; dirty bandages should be replaced with clean ones and washed;

User (2026-10-16): replace the character with a high-poly, textured, realistic human, keeping
the existing skeleton and animations. Audit first: the resident was the original game mesh
(body 916 + shirt 264 + trousers 386 + hair 134 triangles, 256 px textures, Lambert, untextured
hair, no shoes).

Built `tools/build_resident_hd.py` (+ `resident_hd_stages.py`, `resident_hd_dress.py`,
`resident_hd_texture.py`; `npm run character:build`, deterministic, no network):
- Body: the CC0 MakeHuman base mesh and CC0 macro targets from the locally installed MPFB 2.0.17
  extension (male, young adult, slightly muscular, 0.6 Asian / 0.4 European macro blend).
- Repose: MakeHuman's own CC0 game-engine skin weights blend per-bone maps that put each MakeHuman
  joint segment onto the Bip01 bind pose (torso stretch, limb segments with axial stretch, rigid
  hands/feet/head). The weights are then remapped to Bip01 (fingers fold onto Finger0/Finger1),
  so the existing animation sampler drives the new body through the game's own inverse bind
  matrices (the test checks they match).
- Clothing: shells of the body surface (same skin weights, so they move with it), subdivided,
  offset from a Laplacian-smoothed copy of the body so anatomy reads as fabric, kept clear of the
  real skin, with hem/neck/cuff loops relaxed into clean lines. The parts are a crew-neck T-shirt
  with short sleeves, straight-leg jeans and briefs that stay on in the shower. The sneakers are
  lofted superellipse sections sized per foot slice, with a flat sole, rounded toe/heel and an open
  collar. Hair is a scalp shell with an alpha-cut hairline. Eyes are the helper spheres with their
  own iris UVs.
- Textures, painted per texel from a UV-space rest-position map:
  - skin 2048² with mottling, warm cheeks/nose/ears, lips, brows of short strokes, stubble shadow,
    and a pore bump;
  - denim twill with outseam/inseam, waistband, belt loops, pockets, hems and wear;
  - jersey knit with a ribbed collar and twin-needle hems;
  - hair strands radiating from the crown;
  - shoe sole tread, welt, lacing and eyelets;
  - eye iris fibres and limbal ring.
- Size: 61.5k triangles (body 26.8k); 2.8 MB binary + 14.6 KB manifest + 5 MB of textures.

Runtime: `src/resident-hd-character.js` loads the asset and builds one shared Bip01 hierarchy
with SkinnedMesh parts (MeshPhysicalMaterial: sheen cloth, clearcoat eyes, alpha-tested hair). It
copies the sampled source pose each frame (actor facing/mirroring plus bone locals by name).
`volume-characters` uses it for the resident once it has loaded. Zombies and the 2D sprite keep the
imported actor, and `pz.volumeView.characters.setDetail('legacy'|'hd')` compares them. The HD body
is synced as soon as it loads, so it appears even when the game starts paused. In the shower,
clothes and shoes hide and the briefs stay.

Verified in the browser (daylight, same cameras): standing, walking (stride and arm swing),
sitting on the sofa (rest pose), face close-up — `evidence505/character/` hd-* vs legacy-*.
1065/1065 JS tests (new resident-hd suite: completeness, normalised weights, bind matrices equal
the game's, feet/hands/head on the joints, clothing encloses the body, shared skeleton and
name-based sync, shower visibility, materials). Asset freshness exit 0. The rebuild is
byte-identical.

Limits (phase 1): height follows the game skeleton (~1.66 m; the legacy figure reached 1.8 m with
a cartoon head). Fingers move as the skeleton's two finger bones. No facial animation or blinking.
Shoulders of the shirt are a little full. The shower was checked by test, not walked live this
session. Rebuilding needs MPFB installed locally (the built asset is committed-ready in
assets/characters/resident-hd).

## 505l — Sofa, bedding, lampshades and surface materials

User (2026-10-16): do the low-quality furniture and materials next — sofa, bedding,
lampshades, and the fabric, floor, grass and worktop textures. The audit found that fabrics,
walnut, floors and grass were 128 px reprojections of tiny sprite regions (an 18×9 px patch
upscaled for every cushion). Grass was per-pixel noise and the worktop "honed stone" was
high-contrast speckle.

New `src/procedural-surfaces.js` (seamless, deterministic, runtime-generated, cached):
- `oak-floor`, 1024² at 1.4 m: ~14 cm boards, random staggered butt joints per row, per-board
  tone, flat-sawn cathedral grain, along-grain pores and fibre, occasional knots, bevelled
  joints in relief, satin roughness.
- `lawn`, 1024² at 1.2 m: thatch and soil under thin blade strokes, dry tips, clover clusters.
- `quartz`, 1024²: quiet fine aggregate, clouding and two faint wandering veins (no speckle).
- `fabric`, 512² (linen/boucle/percale/basket): yarn-level weave relief, two-ply heather,
  slubs; tinted by the material colour.
- `walnut`, 512²: cathedral figure with open pores.

Recipes switched: 37 linen/cloth materials (chunky linen/boucle upholstery, percale bedding,
waffle duvet, linen shades and curtains), 14 walnut, 3 worktops (counter, sink, breakfast bar),
and the two wood floors plus the yard grass in `surface-materials`. The bathroom tile and paving
keep their source floors. The material cache and architectural validation accept the new kinds.

Geometry:
- Upholstery envelopes render at 22×44 (was 12×24) while collision keeps the coarse 12×24
  hull. Rendered vertices are clamped inside it (after creases), so collision stays compact and
  render ⊆ collision still holds.
- Sectional sofa: base, back and track arms are padded envelopes with sewn piping instead of
  rounded boxes. Feet are turned, tapered walnut with brass ferrules.
- Bed: a quilted duvet falls over both sides and the foot (`quiltedDuvet`: channel quilting,
  loose rumple), with a turned-down fold under seamed pillows (`pillowVolume`: lofted faces
  closing to a seam, pinched ears, head dent). New off-white percale `bedding` material and a
  warm stone duvet (was slate grey and unreadable in the dim bedroom).
- Lamps: box-pleated linen shell, inner lining that glows when lit (new `lining` material),
  rolled hems top and bottom, and a three-arm wire spider (no collision, no self-shadow). Lit
  fabric now glows through its own weave (`bindMaterial` uses the colour map as `emissiveMap`).
  The socket shadow flag is now set by part id, not position.

Verified in the browser at the audit cameras (`evidence505/materials/` before-* vs after-*):
real oak floorboards, piped upholstered sofa with visible weave, readable bed with pillows and
duvet drape, the lit shade showing linen and hems, a clean quartz bar top, and mown lawn.
1071/1071 JS tests (new procedural-surfaces suite: determinism, seamless tiling, oak
joints/hue, quiet quartz, pillow/duvet shape, lamp parts and flags, emissive weave). Volume
deliveries rebuilt; canonical asset build and freshness pass.

Limits: the sofa throw is still the older draped sheet. Paving and bathroom tiles are unchanged.
Fabric relief is bump, not a tangent-space normal map. Shade pleats are subtle at room distance.
Old content-hashed deliveries remain in assets/volume, as before.

## 505m — Every surface textured; world-space variation

User (2026-10-16): overall the materials still look simple, each a single block of colour.
Audit after 505l: 46 material classes (164 recipe materials) still had no texture at all —
dark paint, rubber, stone, ceramic, metal, ivory keys, screens, wood edges, paper, books,
terracotta, leaves, cast iron and more. Even textured ones lacked large-scale variation.

- **World-space variation on every volume surface** (`src/surface-variation.js`, installed by
  `createVolumeSurfaceMaterial`). Albedo is modulated by three octaves of 3-D noise (±10 %,
  broad/mid/fine) and roughness by ±28 % (uneven sheen). It is continuous across meshes, needs
  no UVs and does not tile. Metals vary less; glass, transparent and lamp-emissive materials opt
  out (`variation:false` per material). Shader symbols carry a unique `tsv` prefix: the first
  attempt reused `vTianWorld`/`tianP` from the bounce/parallax patches and blanked every surface
  (caught live, regression-tested).
- **New material-class textures** (`procedural-surfaces.js`):
  - `ceramic`: pooled glaze, faint crazing, pinholes;
  - `paint`: orange-peel relief and roller direction;
  - `plastic`: mould stipple;
  - `paper`: fibre, laid lines, cockle;
  - `terracotta`: grog, pores, firing blush;
  - `leaf`: midrib, parallel veins, waxy sheen;
  - `cast-iron`: granular cast skin;
  - `wall-paint`: 1024² matt emulsion with roller stipple, trowel undulation and coat patchiness;
    it replaces the 256 px plaster on walls.

  Assigned by class across all recipes. Metal, brass and steel get brushed metal; wood, timber
  and edges get walnut grain; stone gets quartz; books get basket-weave cloth; cloth, linen and
  accent get woven fabric. That is 164 materials plus the walls.
- **Character bug** found while checking: the HD resident's left shoe was wound inside-out and
  back-face culled (bare foot). Shoes now orient by signed volume, with a test.

Verified in the browser (`evidence505/materials/pass2-*`): floorboards vary board to board,
the basin shows glaze highlights and tone, the walls have soft paint mottling, and the lamp
shades and woods show texture. 1074/1074 JS tests (new surface-variation suite; two tests
updated for the wall-paint kind and the extra relief map). Volume deliveries and the canonical
asset build regenerated; freshness passes.

Limits: variation is procedural look development, not scanned PBR. There is no edge wear or
cavity grime (no curvature data). The jeans twill can moiré at mid distance. Food props rely on
the global variation only.

## 505n — Commit and performance pass

Committed the 505 work as b860d45 on codex/pz-browser (284 files). The 130 stale
content-hashed volume deliveries left by intermediate rebuilds were removed, and only the
deliveries referenced by build-report were added. The east/west orientation fix stays on its
own branch (`claude/musing-jepsen-b5184e`) pending the user's decision.

Measured in the visible Browser pane. A hidden pane throttles animation frames to about
1 fps, so readings from a hidden pane are invalid.
- Start Game to playable: 9.0 s; JS heap 413 MB.
- After 505m: 415k scene triangles, 409k of them shadow casting. Per frame: 3.56 M triangles,
  2045 draws; CPU p50/p95 16.5/39 ms (render-measure tool).
- Diagnosis by toggling: AO and the HD character barely matter, and pixel ratio makes no
  difference (not fragment-bound). Turning lamps off dropped CPU to 6.9/20 ms and draws to 710:
  the cost was the fixture spot shadow maps, re-rendered every frame (`autoUpdate`).

Changes:
- Shadow level of detail. Dense upholstery envelopes no longer cast; their coarse 12×24 hull
  casts through a depth-only proxy (`shadowProxy`, colour and depth writes off, identity tags
  only, excluded from material patching). Piping, pillows and the quilted duvet don't cast
  (the mattress and cushions do). On the HD resident only the body and shoes cast; clothes and
  hair follow the silhouette within millimetres. Shadow-casting triangles 409k → 250k.
- Shadow scheduling (`volume-fixture-lights`). Fixture shadow maps never auto-update. They
  refresh on rebuild, lamp switch-on and animated fridge doors, drawers and lids
  (`invalidateShadows`). They also refresh when a character whose pose actually changed is
  inside a light's cone and range (`spotTouches`), plus one frame after it leaves. These
  character refreshes are budgeted at two lights per frame, least recently refreshed first.

Result (settled, same view):
- Paused/static: 581–706 draws (was 1975–2045), frame cadence p50/p95 16.7/23 ms (steady 60 fps).
- Running with the resident idle or walking, by a direct frame timer: p50 ≈ 18 ms, p95 31–39 ms.
  That is about the pre-takeover baseline (497: 16.7/25–33 ms). The remaining cost is two
  full-scene passes per frame (main and AO depth/normal) at ~8 ms CPU submission each, not
  shadows. The panel's measure tool forces an extra flush per frame, so while running it
  reads about 31 ms; it is only meaningful for paused views.

Tests: new shadow-scheduling suite (cone test, static rooms skip work, budgeted round-robin,
invalidation, lamps off). The fixture-light test now encodes the scheduled contract.
Diagnostics: `pz.volumeView.sceneStats()` (triangles and shadow triangles per object) and
`pz.volumeView.renderer`.

Not done: halving the per-frame CPU cost needs fewer draw calls (static batching across
objects or a merged static scene) and an AO pass that reuses the main depth; both are larger
refactors.

## 505p — Realistic exterior: sky, landscape, envelope detail, outdoor light

The plot previously floated in a flat clear colour with nothing beyond the lawn, under a blue
metal roof. The exterior is now a place.
- **Sky** (`exterior-sky.js`): three's Preetham `Sky` with drifting clouds, patched for the Z-up
  world. It adds a star dome and a moonlit deep-blue night floor, because Preetham alone goes
  black below the horizon. Linear aerial haze runs 45–650 m. A PMREM environment is captured
  from the sky alone and re-captured when the sun moves more than 2°. The day intensity is
  scaled to 0.35 beside the 3.2 sun; the unscaled sky blew out all exterior colour.
  - The bounce study (cache key v13) gives the sky irradiance to outdoor fragments only.
  - Inside rooms, sky reflections and fill are removed and the room probes stay in charge.
  - The hemisphere light is now a weak 0.4× fill.
- **Landscape** (`exterior-landscape.js`, runtime and seeded, no gameplay):
  - A 1.6 km meadow with the lawn texture and broad variation.
  - A tree line at 55–260 m: 330 deciduous trees (welded, lumpy crowns with underside
    occlusion) and 170 layered, drooping conifers.
  - Four garden trees: a leaning trunk with a root flare, 5–6 scaffold limbs forking to twigs,
    and about 880 alpha-tested leaf cards. The cards face outward from the crown, so the canopy
    shades as one volume, and inner leaves are darker. They cast dappled shadows.
  - A clipped boundary hedge: core plus small-leaf cards, open where the path leaves.
  - About 53k swaying grass blades (3 segments, base-to-tip shade) on lawn within 7 m of the
    house.
  - Shadow, soft-filter and AO hooks are installed on the landscape too; without them the
    shaders exceeded 16 texture units.
- **Envelope** (`exterior-architecture.js`, render-only, derived from walls and roofs):
  - A 0.3 m parapet in the wall finish, plus a folded anodised coping with drip lips. Both hide
    with the roof cutaway.
  - A proud, darker textured-render plinth, broken at door thresholds.
  - Pressed aluminium sills for windows without authored sills.
  - Two downpipes with hopper heads, stand-off brackets and shoes, at outer corners away from
    doors.
- **Roof**: the low-pitch skin is now a gravel-ballast flat roof. `gravel` is a new procedural
  albedo kind (river stones with domed relief and dark membrane gaps); there is no longer a
  metallic sheet.

Checked in the browser:
- Aerial, ground-level, corner close-up (downpipe, plinth, gravel over the coping), 17:15 low
  sun with dappled tree shadow on the wall, 18:10 horizon glow, and night (stars, blue dome,
  lit windows).
- The resident's cutaway game view: parapets hide and the interior is unchanged.
- Cost: about 1.1M triangles in the main pass (tree line 297k, grass 318k after LOD trims).

Tests: 1100/1100. New suites: `exterior-architecture` and `exterior-scene` (sky mapping and
night, plot layout and hedge gap, tree crowns, gravel). The lighting test now uses the weaker
hemisphere fill, and the surface-material test expects gravel. Volume deliveries were
regenerated and the asset check passes.

Open:
- Sunlit white walls sit near the top of the AgX curve.
- The tree line is instanced but has no billboard LOD.
- The 18:00 sunset is abrupt: the artistic cycle sets at exactly 18:00.

## 505q — Exterior review pass: shaded walls, garden planting, vegetation shading

Reviewed the exterior from a set of fixed views and fixed what looked wrong. Evidence:
`evidence505/exterior/505q-*.jpg`. The shots were captured from the canvas after the
composer draws, so they also work while the browser pane is hidden (dev helper
`test-results/exterior-view.js`, `__ext.shot`).

- **Walls in shade looked olive-khaki and turned black with the bounce study off.**
  - Cause: once the bounce study assigns a room reflection capture to a wall, its outer face
    used that interior capture as if it were sky (the compile-time `TIAN_SKY_ENV` flag was
    stale).
  - Fix: a live per-draw `tianSkyEnv` uniform. Outdoor fragments of materials with their own
    capture drop that radiance and take the sky irradiance as order-2 SH instead (`SKY_SH`,
    computed by `exterior-sky.js` from a 16 px cube of the sky alone).
  - Bounce cache key is now v15. Interiors are unchanged (checked with the bounce recaptured).
- **Planted borders** inside the hedge:
  - Shredded-bark mulch beds with steel edging.
  - Clipped box balls: a leaf-mat core plus about 120 outward-facing cards.
  - Drifts of lavender, daisy and rudbeckia (new procedural cards).
- **Rough meadow** beyond the hedge: about 6,500 swaying tufts with seed heads, thinning with
  distance. The meadow ground is now olive with broad patches.
- **Garden trees**:
  - A continuous trunk that flares into five buttress roots.
  - Procedural furrowed-plate bark: albedo plus bump, meandering furrows, lichen on ridges.
- **Hedge core** is a dense opaque leaf texture instead of a flat box colour.
- **Vegetation shading**:
  - Foliage, grass, the lawn surface (recipe `fibre.specular` 0.22) and mulch use a low
    specular level; grazing sky reflection had washed them out to pale grey.
  - The ambient-occlusion pass now skips alpha-tested meshes. Its override material ignored
    the cut-outs, so every leaf and flower card occluded as a dark rectangle.
- **Bugs found on the way**:
  - Card UVs: pixel generators write rows top-down, so plants rendered upside down. The v
    coordinate is now flipped; tested.
  - Lawn blades fade out from 4 m to 8 m, so the sward has no visible edge.

Tests: 1103/1103. Volume deliveries were regenerated and the asset check passes.

Open:
- The tree line is still smooth blobs up close.
- Blank long walls; the generic door leaf.
- Lawn blades cover only the area around the house.

## 505s — Toward a TLOU2-grade exterior: image, weathering, foliage, ground, glass

Asked to judge the exterior against The Last of Us Part II. It was far off: walls ghosted the
interior through, everything was factory-clean, the sky was white, and the ground and horizon
were flat. This pass works on the cues that sell a photographic image. Evidence:
`evidence505/exterior/505s-*.jpg`, including `505s-before-ghosting.jpg`.

- **Wall ghosting (bug)**:
  - The wall and furniture fade systems left materials permanently in the transparent pass.
  - A recompile then stalled the frame loop on a fade tail (opacity ≈ 0.98, depth writes off),
    so interior objects drew over walls.
  - Fix: transparency only while actually fading, depth kept until 30% faded, the last 2%
    snaps, and the loop resumes when a material compile finishes.
- **Camera image** (`cinematic-grade.js`, in the composer before AgX):
  - Warm daylight white balance, log-space filmic contrast, saturation 1.1, cool-shadow /
    warm-highlight split tone, vignette and luminance-weighted grain.
  - Bloom limited to genuine highlights (threshold 25 in scene-linear).
- **Exposure**:
  - The exterior is now exposed for sunlight (0.72; a shaded white render reads light grey).
    Rooms keep their brightness via a compensating interior gain.
  - Seen from the garden in daylight, interiors drop about 55% (smoothed), so windows read as
    dark glass. The roof-cutaway game view is unaffected.
- **Sky**:
  - The visible dome is shown at 0.5 of the radiance it lights with, so it reads blue.
  - Night environment fill is 0.16, and moonlight is 1.2 (was 0.5) so a moonlit garden stays
    readable.
  - Aerial haze now runs 90–1100 m.
- **Weathering** (`facade-weathering.js`, outdoor fragments only, tested by room boxes):
  - Rain run-off streaks hang from the coping, each to its own length.
  - Splash-back soil and algae band at the foot of walls; broad uneven weathering.
  - Grime and damp where paving meets walls.
  - Roughness follows the dirt.
- **Ground and horizon**:
  - Lawn and meadow get dry/yellow and lush patches.
  - The meadow is olive and rolls (±3 m swells beyond a 12 m apron), and tree line and tufts
    follow the terrain.
  - Forested hills ring the plot at 380–1100 m.
  - About 16k meadow tufts thin with distance; leaf litter lies under the garden trees.
- **Foliage**:
  - Back-lit leaf translucency (wrapped transmission on the key light) on tree, hedge, flower
    and tuft cards.
  - Leaf wind: gust sway rising with height, plus flutter.
  - Denser canopies (17 cards per twig) and hedge tops without a 1 m grid.
  - Tree-line crowns are darker with more cards; conifers are dark with needle sprays at the
    silhouette, in matt materials.
- **Glass**:
  - Opacity follows Fresnel (clear face-on, mirror at grazing angles).
  - Panes reflect a per-build PMREM panorama of the garden taken from the plot centre with
    the house hidden, not just the sky.

Tests: 1111/1111. New `facade-weathering` suite: vector uniform arrays, reserved-word guard,
Fresnel glass, ground variation, foliage injection. New cutaway test: an opaque wall stays in
the opaque pass. Lighting and daylight tests are updated for the brighter moon. The asset
check passes. Interior views checked after a bounce recapture: unchanged.

## 505t — Vegetation rebuilt from the leaf up

"Plants still look fake, especially on the walls." The cause was the technique itself: every
plant was flat cards painted with identical ellipse leaves. The wall climber read as a topiary
blob glued to the render, with no stems, no wall between leaves, no veins, no gloss and no
contact shadow. Evidence: `evidence505/exterior/505t-*.jpg` (`ivy-before` vs `ivy-after`).

- **`vegetation-leaves.js`** (new):
  - `leafTexture` generates one leaf per species: ovate serrated tree leaf, five-lobed ivy,
    glossy box. Each has midrib and secondary (or palmate) veins, mottling, a paler margin,
    a darker base, an antialiased alpha edge and a tangent-space normal map (domed blade,
    sunken veins). Species colours are linear and written sRGB-encoded; the first cut wrote
    them raw and every leaf came out about 5× too dark.
  - `clusterCard` composites many such leaves into a card, with per-leaf rotation, layer
    shading and normals rotated into card space. A wrapped variant tiles.
  - `leafGeometry` is a curved blade mesh (V-fold along the midrib, tip curl).
  - `growClimber` grows a vine: main stems from the root, side shoots along each trellis
    wire, arching laterals, and alternate leaves at nodes facing out and up. Old wood carries
    larger, darker leaves; tips are small and fresh.
- **Ivy on blank walls**:
  - Around 1,100 individual curved ivy leaves per wall, as instanced meshes with leaf
    normal maps, gloss, translucency and slight wind.
  - Stems are swept bark tubes, green on young shoots.
  - Soft contact-shadow decals sit on the render behind each leaf. AO cannot do this for
    cut-outs: opting the leaves into AO left square halos, so that was reverted.
- **Garden trees**:
  - Branches are now single smooth swept tubes along curving paths (parallel-transport
    frames, taper), with laterals leaving along their length; there are no straight jointed
    sticks.
  - Bark is warmer.
  - The crown uses clusters of real veined leaves; about 3,000 cards per tree make a full,
    lower crown.
- **Hedge**: clusters of privet-sized box leaves with normal maps, and a tiling dense-leaf
  core; low gloss.
- **Box balls**: lumpy cores in a darker "inner depth" tone under about 420 leaf-cluster
  cards each.
- **Tree line**: crowns built mostly from about 240 leaf-cluster cards per tree, so cards
  make the silhouette; a smaller core sits deep inside.
- **Leaf litter**: clusters of dried, browned real leaves.
- **Lawn blades**: darkened to sit in the sward (they read as pale hairs before).
- **Robustness**: a sanitise pass before bloom replaces NaN/Inf pixels. One transient NaN
  had been smeared over the whole frame by the bloom blur, blanking it. Unlit decal materials
  are kept out of the shadow-atlas and AO hooks (the atlas hook threw on a basic material).

Cost: the frame stays at about 3.45M triangles across all passes. Tests: 1115/1115 (new
`vegetation-leaves` suite: species outlines and colour encoding, unit normals, ivy lobes,
tiling clusters, blade mesh and orientation, climber growth). The asset check passes.

## 505u — Scanned CC0 assets (Poly Haven): import pipeline, gravel roof, bark, mulch, real plants

With the user's approval (14 assets, about 99 MB), photographic scans were downloaded from
Poly Haven (CC0 1.0) into `private-assets/polyhaven/`. These sources are not committed;
SHA-256 for every file is in `source-manifest.json`.
- **Pipeline**:
  - `tools/build_scanned_assets.py` (`npm run assets:scanned` / `assets:scanned:check`)
    verifies source hashes and builds `assets/scanned/`: 1K colour, 1K normal and packed
    AO/roughness JPEGs per texture.
  - glTF models are copied byte-for-byte, with their textures re-encoded at the size the
    recipe records.
  - `assets/scanned/manifest.json` records author, source page, licence, source hashes and
    delivery hashes. The recipe is `assets/recipes/scanned/polyhaven.json`.
  - The import is a raster/mesh conversion of scans, not a procedural reconstruction.
- **Runtime**: `src/scanned-assets.js` provides `scannedTextureSet` and `loadScannedModel`
  (Y-up to Z-up, recipe scale, sits on z=0).
- **Material kind**: surface recipes can use `texture.kind:'scanned'`. The roof is now
  `gravel_floor_02`.
- **Varying limit**: scanned materials use colour and normal only. The extra per-map UV
  varyings, on top of the bounce, weathering and variation world-position varyings,
  exceeded the GPU limit ("could not pack varying"), so a material silently failed to
  render.
- **Landscape**:
  - Garden-tree bark uses `bark_willow`; bed mulch uses `wood_chips`.
  - Scanned plants are instanced: `shrub_04`/`shrub_03` and `periwinkle_plant` in the
    borders, `shrub_02` near plot corners, `fern_02` and `weed_plant_02` at the house foot,
    `dandelion_01` in the lawn margins, and `garden_hose_wall_mounted_01` on the first long
    blank wall.
  - Models arrive asynchronously; `installLandscapeMaterials` (panel) installs the shadow
    and AO hooks on late arrivals.
- **Dropped**:
  - The scanned pavers: the asset is mosaic-scale and the patio keeps its large slabs.
  - Scanned trees and grass clumps (1–4.6M polygons each).
- **Parallel session**: it simplified the scanned instancing to a triangle budget and
  reverted the meadow to the procedural lawn (the scan read as soil from the game camera).

Tests: new `scanned-assets` suite (provenance, delivery hashes, runtime lists, size budget,
roof recipe). Full suite 1123/1123 on the combined working tree. The asset check passes.

## 505v — A lived-in place: surroundings, weather, light, life and LOD

"Do it all" on the gap list after 505t, which named six areas: lived-in details, surroundings,
lighting, vegetation, life and weather, and LOD. Evidence: `evidence505/exterior/505v-*.jpg`.
Before/after pairs: `step-before`/`step-after` and `hills-before`/`wide`.

- **Lived-in props** (`exterior-props.js`, new). Built from merged meshes per material.
  - At every exterior door: a dark steel canopy on tie rods, a concrete step, a coir mat, a
    letterbox and a number plaque.
  - Around the plot: a wall tap with hose reel, a bicycle leaning on a facade, a washing line
    with draped cloth, a wheelbarrow, a spade and a watering can.
  - Doors build in a mirrored frame (t = out rotated clockwise). Mirroring reverses the
    triangle winding, so the step and canopy showed their insides as phantom upstands. `oriented`
    now restores the winding whenever the basis determinant is negative.
  - Wall-hung props stay out of GTAO, which blackened them against the shaded render.
- **Surroundings** (`exterior-context.js`, new).
  - A terrain-following lane on the gate side, with kerbs and an asphalt texture.
  - A gravel drive and an open five-bar gate on posts.
  - Timber poles every 38 m carrying three sagging wires, with a drop wire to the house.
  - Four neighbouring cottages (gabled, with chimney, windows and hedge).
  - Worn-path decals and weeds in the paving joints.
  - `blocked(x, y)` keeps tufts, trees and wildflowers off the lane, drive and cottages.
- **Lighting**.
  - Ground bounce: sunlit lawn green lifts the undersides of outdoor surfaces in
    `room-bounce-study`.
  - Screen-space sun shafts, as a pass between the NaN sanitiser and bloom.
  - Contact-AO decals under trees and props, and soft decals under the tree line, stand in for
    far-shadow AO.
- **Vegetation**.
  - Tree-line variety: five species and size variants, with retries around blocked ground.
  - One gust-front field drives grass, tufts and leaves together (`leafFront`).
  - About 1,400 wildflowers and joint weeds.
  - Instanced vegetation is chunked (blades 8 m / 45 m, tufts and flowers 20 m / 160 m) for
    frustum and distance culling.
  - Scanned plants are decimated to `SCAN_BUDGET` with `SimplifyModifier`, after merging
    vertices; without it the renderer reached about 13M triangles.
- **Weather and life** (`atmosphere-effects.js`, new).
  - Deterministic daily weather (`weatherFor`): clear, overcast, or afternoon rain.
  - Cloud shadows drift across every lit material.
  - Rain falls as 3,500 streaks; the streaks dim with daylight, where they were white at night.
  - Wetness soaks and dries over time: it darkens and glosses up-facing surfaces and pools
    puddles on hard ground.
  - Heavy cover greys and hazes the sky, dims the sun and hides the stars.
  - A forced weather mode (`weather.set`) now applies immediately; it used to wait for the
    next clock tick.
  - Birds circle overhead and leaves fall from the garden trees.
- **Distant hills**. The ring of hills 0.4–1 km out now draws pre-lit in aerial perspective:
  woodland blue-green and paler pasture, lifted toward the haze and dimmed with daylight. Under
  full lighting the bright dome had washed them to a grey band, and at night they glowed.
- **Character**. The resident's body atlas has transparent lower corners (PZ masks the legs
  under clothing). Drawn opaque, they showed as a dark patch on the back of the trousers.
  Character materials now alpha-test their textures (`characters.js`).

Scanned-asset hookups in `exterior-landscape.js` and `workshop-volume-panel.js` (scanned
ground, bark and mulch; section 10 plant instancing; the `onChange` hook and generation guard;
`installLandscapeMaterials`) are the 505u session's work. They are committed here because both
files carry hunks from both sessions.

Cost: the night view with every lamp shadow measures about 8.5M triangles across all passes,
and a daytime view about 2.7–3.5M. Tests: 1123/1123 (new `exterior-life` suite: props
placement, context blocking, chunked instancing and cut-out normals, weather determinism). The
asset check passes.
